# Linux Syscall Compliance Matrix

Primary source: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/master/arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` (`arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl`, native `common`/`64` rows only; `x32` rows excluded).
Local cross-check: `/usr/src/kernels/6.19.6-100.fc42.x86_64/arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl`.

Generation rule: syscall numbers, ABI tags, names, and Linux entry points come from Linux source. Oxide source is used only for current-route annotation and subsystem impact mapping.

Generated rows: 385. Current branch annotation: `B903-network-packet-tx-poll`.
Local cross-check delta: missing-from-local=471:rseq_slice_yield; extra-local=none.

## Status Legend

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `NEEDS-AUDIT` | Linux has a real entry; Oxide has a visible route, but semantic parity is not proven in this matrix. |
| `PARTIAL` | A committed fix/audit exists for part of the Linux contract, but the row still has remaining semantic or harness gaps. |
| `IMPL` | Full Linux-row semantics are implemented and backed by the required harness evidence. |
| `DISPATCH-GAP` | Linux has a real entry; Oxide lacks a visible `NR_*` constant or dispatch route. |
| `LINUX-ENOSYS` | Linux source intentionally has no real native implementation for this slot. Oxide must return `-ENOSYS`, not a stub success. |
| `IN-PROGRESS` | Local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall family. |

## Audit Rules

| Rule | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Row authority | Add/remove/renumber rows only by re-reading Linux `syscall_64.tbl`. |
| Semantic authority | Verify behavior against Linux implementation source named in `Linux refs`, not against local notes. |
| Harness authority | A row is not complete until its required harness covers success, error ordering, permissions, ownership, namespace, symlink/hardlink, tmpfs/dev/proc/sysfs, and user-copy fault cases relevant to its systems. |
| No fallback truth | Oxide route presence only means code is reachable; it is not evidence of Linux compatibility. |

## B825 Cross-Cutting Evidence

|---|---|---|---|
| `16:ioctl` | `IN-PROGRESS` | Socket-fd SIOC dispatch uses the socket-captured network namespace and owner-user-namespace `CAP_NET_ADMIN`; `SIOCADDRT`/`SIOCDELRT` validate Linux `rtentry`, mutate the canonical FIB atomically, and return `EEXIST`/`ESRCH`; `SIOCGIFCONF` enumerates namespace-owned IPv4 addresses without fixed ifindex scans. | Complete mutable MTU, hardware-address, broadcast-address, and transmit-queue ioctl state; replace raw fixed-offset user reads with the shared fault-recoverable ABI import path; direct syscall-context fault/order coverage. |
| `41-55` INET socket family | existing row status retained | Socket-owned IPv4/IPv6 UDP/TCP, multicast memberships, PMTU/errors, implicit bind, diagnostics, send routing, and receive demux carry the captured or ingress-derived network namespace. IPv4/IPv6 fragment keys include namespace identity. `/proc/net/*`, rtnetlink dumps/mutations/multicast, and inet-diag use per-open or socket-captured namespace state. Hosted evidence: `net` 423, `netlink` 59, `nscg` 10, `procfs` 44, complete `syscalls` test set. | Canonical refcounted network-namespace ownership and last-reference teardown remains a separate architecture change; existing per-namespace cleanup primitives alone are not completion evidence. Existing row-specific syscall ABI, protocol-family, security-hook, and differential gaps remain. |

## B869 Cross-Cutting Evidence

| Rows | Status | Evidence | Remaining |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0:read`, `45:recvfrom`, `47:recvmsg`, `299:recvmmsg` | existing row status retained | Physical and loopback receive delivery now acquires the exact interface generation before dequeue/dispatch. Linux NAPI and skb work carries that generation through `netif_rx`; Virtio additionally requires exact device-Arc identity, rejecting equal-generation device-key reuse. Namespace final-drop completion publishes only after owner fields drop, and loopback retirement closes admission before purging. B869 proves ingress owner/generation lifetime only; it does not promote any row status. |

| 0 | common | `read` | `sys_read` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, procfs, sched, socket, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:23` | `IMPL` | `B719-syscall-000-read-complete; B853-vsock-final-file-release` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(read), fs/read_write.c:ksys_read, fs/read_write.c:vfs_read, fs/read_write.c:rw_verify_area, include/linux/fs.h:MAX_RW_COUNT, net/socket.c:sock_read_iter, net/socket.c:sock_recvmsg`; B854-socket-file-ownership; B868-network-blocked-io-close | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_read_shape.rs; vfs::file_read; net hosted suite; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `read(2)` audit against local Linux source. Syscall fd lookup now precedes user-buffer validation; no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd return `EBADF` before `EFAULT`; `FMODE_READ` rejects write-only fds before touching the user buffer; zero-length reads still validate fd/mode and call the backend; original count is user-access validated before Linux `MAX_RW_COUNT` clamp; backend sees the clamped count; negative-count-shaped u64 counts fault through access validation before clamp, matching Linux access_ok-before-rw_verify ordering on x86_64. VSOCK read now stays on the originally pinned File through VFS dispatch, so fd close/reuse cannot redirect the operation. Netlink read stays on the netlink receive queue path. Socket VFS read no longer returns blanket `EINVAL`: stream sockets use stream readers, datagram/packet sockets consume one message through the same receive core as `recvfrom(..., src=NULL)`, and fresh/listening TCP returns `ENOTCONN` like Linux. VFS read hooks still emit access notification for positive reads; syscall-level task I/O accounting now updates `rchar`/`syscr`, and procfs exposes dynamic `/proc/<pid>/io` plus `/proc/self/io`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_read_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs file_read`; `cargo test -p net`; `cargo check -p fs`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. B854 keeps NETLINK read on the same pinned File through queue dispatch and proves close/reuse cannot retarget the operation. B868 adds lock-coupled terminal receive waits for VSOCK, NETLINK, and AF_UNIX while retaining the original File across close/reuse. |
| 1 | common | `write` | `sys_write` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:24` | `IMPL` | `B720-syscall-001-write-complete` | `fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(write), fs/read_write.c:ksys_write, fs/read_write.c:vfs_write, fs/read_write.c:rw_verify_area, fs/read_write.c:generic_write_check_limits, fs/read_write.c:generic_write_checks; fs/super.c:freeze_super, fs/super.c:sb_start_write, fs/super.c:thaw_super; include/asm-generic/access_ok.h:__access_ok; net/socket.c:sock_write_iter; net/ipv4/udp.c:udp_sendmsg; net/unix/af_unix.c:unix_dgram_sendmsg`; B868-network-blocked-io-close | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_write_shape.rs; vfs::sb_freeze_write; vfs::file_write_limits; net hosted suite; netlink VFS write harness; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `write(2)` audit against local Linux source. Syscall fd lookup precedes user-buffer validation; no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd return `EBADF` before `EFAULT`; `FMODE_WRITE` rejects read-only/O_PATH fds before touching the buffer; zero-length writes validate fd/mode and call the backend; original count is user-access validated before Linux `MAX_RW_COUNT` clamp; backend sees the clamped count. Source buffer now prevalidates a readable VMA to avoid kernel page faults until extable copy-from-user recovery exists; target checks compile the real path. Task I/O accounting updates `wchar`/`syscw`; existing VFS hooks update mtime/ctime and inotify for positive writes. Prior B715 work completed RLIMIT_FSIZE, `s_maxbytes`, socket, datagram, IPv6 UDP, AF_UNIX, and netlink write parity. Freeze is now Linux-shaped: new regular-file writers sleep while frozen; thaw wakes them; `freeze_super` drains existing writers before `freeze_fs`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_write_shape`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_read_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs --test sb_freeze_write`; `cargo test -p vfs --test superblock_freeze`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_write_limits`; `cargo test -p net`; `cargo test -p netlink vfs_write_dispatches_netlink_request_and_queues_reply`; `cargo check -p fs`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. B868 adds bounded AF_UNIX send queues and lock-coupled TCP/UNIX writer waits with terminal state published before wake. |
| 2 | common | `open` | `sys_open` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:25` | `IMPL` | `B721-syscall-002-open-complete` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/open.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(open), fs/open.c:do_sys_open, fs/open.c:build_open_how, fs/open.c:build_open_flags, include/linux/file.h:FD_ADD, fs/file.c:get_unused_fd_flags, fs/file.c:fd_install, fs/namei.c:do_open, fs/namei.c:may_open, fs/namei.c:path_openat` | `vfs::install_open_hooks; vfs::fdtable_reserve_install; vfs::file_opath_ops; vfs::file_mnt_readonly; vfs::path_total_max; vfs::path_name_max; fs_syscall_model::syscall_shape_covers_udev_runtime_and_user_permissions; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `open(2)` audit against upstream Linux source. Legacy `open` delegates to shared `openat(AT_FDCWD, path, flags, mode)` like Linux `do_sys_open`; prior B716 work completed `build_open_how` legacy masking, `O_PATH` stripping, `O_TMPFILE`, `O_CREAT|O_DIRECTORY`, `O_DIRECTORY` lookup, sync flag normalization, existing `O_CREAT` directory `EISDIR`, `OPENAT2_REGULAR`, and sticky-dir existing-target checks. This branch fixed the remaining fd-install path to match Linux `FD_ADD`: reserve fd with `get_unused_fd_flags` before open side effects, publish with `fd_install`, rollback with `put_unused_fd` on every failure, and return `EMFILE` before `f_op->open`/truncate side effects. It also removed the syscall-layer pre-call to `inode->i_fop->open`; `install_open_at` now owns the single Linux `do_dentry_open` open hook, skips it for `O_PATH`, exposes the final `File` to `on_open_file`, and propagates `O_TRUNC` backend errors before fd publication. Shared pathname fetch preserves Linux `EFAULT`/`ENOENT`/`ENAMETOOLONG` and byte-count semantics through `read_user_path` and VFS path gates. Evidence: `cargo test -p vfs --test install_open_hooks`; `cargo test -p vfs --test fdtable_reserve_install`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_opath_ops`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_mnt_readonly`; `cargo test -p vfs --test path_total_max`; `cargo test -p vfs --test path_name_max`; `cargo test -p fs --test fs_syscall_model`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. |

The syscall restores the arch `rt_sigframe` register image through HAL, prevalidates the user frame range against readable VMAs before kernel reads, restores the signal mask through Linux-shaped `set_current_blocked` so `SIGKILL`/`SIGSTOP` remain unblockable, and maps malformed frames to forced `SIGSEGV` instead of a returned errno. aarch64 `sigcontext.__reserved` now matches the 4096-byte Linux UAPI field, x86_64/aarch64 frame-range helpers keep layout authority in HAL, and aarch64 restore uses the same per-task SVC-frame slot as delivery. |

Linux fs/ioctl.c:549-554 keeps regular-file FIONREAD in the common path, fs/pipe.c:716-735 handles pipe FIONREAD, and socket protocol handlers use SIOCINQ/SIOCOUTQ through net/socket.c:3570-3602. Evidence: cargo test -q -p fs --test sys_ioctl_queue_count_shape -- --nocapture. B801 fixes Linux block-device read-only status ioctl recognition: BLKROGET now writes the canonical Oxide block-device read-write state (0) through the block ioctl path before the generic non-char-device ENOTTY fallback, matching Linux block/ioctl.c:681-682 and the request number in include/uapi/linux/fs.h:269-270; this is kept consistent with /sys/block/<dev>/ro, which already reports 0 for published disks. B799 fixes Linux unsupported fileattr ioctl errno parity: VFS default `i_op->fileattr_get`/`i_op->fileattr_set` now reports absent ioctl support as `ENOTTY`, so `FS_IOC_GETFLAGS`, `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`, `FS_IOC_FSGETXATTR`, and `FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` on filesystems without fileattr ops match Linux `fs/file_attr.c:84-94,276-285` absent-op `ENOIOCTLCMD` plus `fs/ioctl.c:610-620` syscall fallback to `vfs_ioctl` user-visible `ENOTTY`, while ext4 backend `EOPNOTSUPP` for unsupported advertised attrs remains unchanged. B775 fixes ext4 project-id i_version parity: project-id changes through FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR now force an in-core i_version bump in ext4_fileattr_setproject after the Linux ext4_ioctl_setflags bump, matching ext4_ioctl_setproject's inode_inc_iversion path. Harness first failed with one bump and now proves the Linux two-bump path on the image-backed ext4 mount. B774 fixes ext4 filesystem label ioctls: `FS_IOC_GETFSLABEL`/`FS_IOC_SETFSLABEL` now route through filesystem `unlocked_ioctl`, use Linux request numbers, copy the ext4 17-byte label interface, enforce `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` before `SETFSLABEL` usercopy, reject labels without a NUL in the first 17 bytes, zero-pad and journal `s_volume_name`, restamp the superblock checksum, and expose same-mount plus remount-visible labels. The image harness proves in-core ctime, on-disk ctime, remount persistence, and i_version advance. B770 fixes Linux ext4 project-inherit semantics surrounding `FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR`: `ext4_new_inode` now inherits `i_projid` and Linux `EXT4_FL_INHERITED` flags from project-inherit directories, and ext4 hardlink/rename/exchange reject cross-project moves into `PROJINHERIT` directories with `EXDEV`, matching `fs/ext4/ialloc.c` and `fs/ext4/namei.c`. B769 fixes Linux queue-count ioctl parity: regular-file `FIONREAD` stays `i_size - f_pos`, while non-char `FIONREAD`/`SIOCOUTQ` now routes through `vfs::IoctlIntCmd` and `FileOps::ioctl_int` instead of a generic `poll()` boolean/zero fallback. Pipe/FIFO `FIONREAD` reports queued bytes; inet/unix/packet sockets, netlink, and vsock report backend queue counts where the local model has them; unsupported backends return `ENOTTY` before copyout. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_queue_count_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs pipe -- --nocapture`; `cargo check -p vfs`; `cargo check -p net --features hosted`; `cargo check -p netlink`; `cargo check -p fs`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 VDSO remains blocked by missing `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc`. B768 fixes Linux `fs/ioctl.c` `FIOASYNC` parity: `ioctl_fioasync` reads the user int, treats same-state requests as success, and changes `FASYNC` only through the open file's snapshotted `file_operations::fasync`; unsupported state changes now return `ENOTTY` without changing flags or generic-registering async delivery. VFS now owns a real `FileOps::fasync_file` hook, with pipe/FIFO and socket/netlink/vsock backends opting into the generic fasync registry. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_fasync_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_fowner -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs pipe -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B767 fixes Linux `fs/ioctl.c` default-arm regular-file gate for `FIBMAP` and legacy `FS_IOC_*SP` preallocation ioctls: only regular files now take the regular-file `file_ioctl` path; directories, symlinks, and other non-regular files fall through without usercopy or backend `bmap`/`fallocate` side effects. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_regular_gate_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B766 fixes Linux `FIOQSIZE` parity from `fs/ioctl.c`: accepted directories/regular files/symlinks now return allocated inode bytes (`i_blocks * 512`, matching local `inode_get_bytes` shape) rather than logical size, with hosted coverage for regular sparse-shaped files, directories, symlinks, and special-file `ENOTTY` without user copyout. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_fioqsize_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test fileattr_prepare -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B765 fixes ext4 fileattr admission from Linux `ext4_fileattr_set`: unsupported translated flags are rejected before the backend masks to user-modifiable bits, so unsupported DAX through `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`/`FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` no longer succeeds silently or mutates nothing. B764 fixes ext4 fileattr parity from Linux `ext4_ioctl_check_immutable` and `EXT4_FL_USER_VISIBLE`: immutable ext4 inodes now reject other flag/project-id changes while immutable remains set, `EXT4_EXTENTS_FL` is visible through `FS_IOC_GETFLAGS`, normal flag updates preserve it, and unsupported extent-layout toggles fail instead of clearing the on-disk extent bit. B763 adds Linux-shaped ext4 PROJECT RO_COMPAT admission plus journaled `i_projid` and ctime persistence for `FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` on PROJECT filesystems, with in-memory PROJECT-bit image harness coverage. Prior B734-B744 covered common VFS dispatch, fileattr, remap admission, ext4 no-remap proof, and tty exclusive ioctls. Remaining before `IMPL`: filesystem-specific DAX/casefold support where supported by the backing filesystem, filesystem-specific remap backends only for filesystems that actually advertise Linux remap/reflink support, and remaining driver/file-specific ioctl families. |

## B865 Non-Network Namespace Ownership Evidence

| Rows | Ownership evidence | Remaining row scope |
|---|---|---|
| 56 `clone`, 57 `fork`, 58 `vfork`, 435 `clone3` | Clone core inherits one retained `TaskNamespaceSnapshot`; requested non-network `CLONE_NEW*` replacements allocate canonical `namespace_identity::NamespaceRef` owners, mount namespaces retain `vfs::mntns::MntNamespaceRef`, and `CLONE_NEWPID` publishes exact child/ancestor PID mappings only after fallible setup. `clone_without_new_flags_inherits_exact_owners`, `clone_replaces_every_supported_nonnetwork_owner_and_final_release_drops_them`, and scheduler nested-PID tests prove inheritance, isolation, rollback, PID-for-children transition, and weak ancestor identity behavior. | Existing `PARTIAL` gaps remain; `CLONE_NEWTIME` returns `EINVAL` pending e.iv clock semantics. |
| 60 `exit`, 61 `wait4`, 62 `kill`, 109 `setpgid`, 110 `getppid` | Tasks own exact PID namespace ancestry; PID lookup mappings retain weak namespace identities and cannot retarget after final owner drop. `mark_done_releases_all_nonnetwork_membership_before_zombie` observes the namespace finalizer while the task is still `Runnable`, then proves `Zombie` publication and empty task/mount namespace snapshots; wait/kill/pgrp/parent lookups therefore use retained task or zombie PID snapshots without extending task namespace membership. | Ownership evidence does not close the rows' existing wait, signal, process-group, parent, permission, or errno-order audits. |
| 63 `uname`, 170 `sethostname`, 171 `setdomainname` | UTS names are keyed by the exact canonical UTS owner, not a numeric side table; the owner finalizer removes state. `clones_share_one_owner_state`, `exact_owners_are_isolated`, `owner_kind_and_init_are_validated`, `final_owner_drop_removes_state`, and nsfd-only UTS retention prove shared state, isolation, validation, and final cleanup. | Existing full syscall validation, permission, usercopy, and errno-order audits remain. |
| 29-31 and 64-71 SysV IPC | `ipc::IpcOwner` validates and retains the task's exact canonical IPC `NamespaceRef`; sem, msg, shm, and POSIX-mqueue stores key by that owner's `NamespaceId`, with finalizers reaping owner state. `typed_owner_rejects_non_ipc_identity_without_substitution` and `exact_owner_controls_sharing_isolation_and_final_cleanup` prove no fallback owner, same-owner sharing, cross-owner isolation, and final cleanup. | Statuses remain unchanged; B865 establishes namespace ownership only, not unlisted syscall semantics. |
| 165 `mount`, 166 `umount2` | Syscall entry snapshots the exact caller `MntNamespaceRef`; reservations, graft/copy mutations, commit/abort transactions, procfs open snapshots, and namespace live indexes retain canonical owners. `mntns_ownership` stress proves owner retention through success and rollback; `open_tree_mntns_ownership` proves a detached tree retains owner A and atomically rebinds every node to owner B on attach. | Existing mount-flag, filesystem, `MNT_EXPIRE`, `MNT_FORCE`, propagation, and errno-order gaps remain. |
| 272 `unshare` | Real `sys_unshare` hosted entry tests prove all supported non-network owner replacements, PID-for-children behavior, second pending PID `EINVAL`, rollback without owner leak, and `ESRCH` after terminal task namespace release. B886 also proves `CLONE_FILES` publishes a distinct table while retaining shared file descriptions and isolates subsequent close. TIME requests return `EINVAL` without mutation pending e.iv. | Existing thread/fs/sysvsem/capability and broader Linux ordering audits remain. |
| 308 `setns` | `NsInode` retains a typed exact owner; `setns_from_fd` pins one `Arc<File>` before close/reuse. Per-kind tests cover cgroup, IPC, PID, PID-for-children, user, UTS, mount, and TIME nsfds: fd reuse cannot retarget the pin, final close/drop removes weak live indexes and owner state, and incomplete TIME install returns `EINVAL` without owner mutation. | Existing pidfd multi-namespace, permission, commit-order, and e.iv TIME semantics remain. |
| 470 `listns` | B867 replaces task/inode discovery with one canonical eight-kind monotonic ID registry and atomic active global/per-kind/direct-owner indexes. Separate active references and lifetime pins make nsfd/listns snapshots retain allocation without extending activity; mount/network concrete identities and owner activity cascades use the same registry. Extension/reserved validation, visibility, owner filtering, nsfd-only entries, cursor pagination, empty pages, per-element faults, and snapshot/final-drop schedules pass. | `IMPL`; follow-on work may broaden differential stress but no known row contract is intentionally omitted. |

## B887 AF_PACKET V3 Private-Width Evidence

| Rows | Implemented evidence | Remaining row scope |
|---|---|---|
| 9 `mmap`, 54 `setsockopt` | Linux 6.19 source and host runtime disprove the queued V3 private-offset widening: the kernel validates the full `u32` request but stores `blk_sizeof_priv` as `unsigned short`. A request of 65,536 is accepted and exposes private and first-packet offsets of 48 on Linux and Oxide. Hosted boundary tests cover 65,535/65,536/65,537 plus full-width validation failures; the portable 80-record GNU/glibc differential matches this record exactly. Hosted net passes 854/854 and both GNU targets compile. | Existing non-AF_PACKET mmap and setsockopt row gaps remain. N07.10 retains loopback packet classification, duplicate V3 publication, expanded differential cases, aarch64 execution, and integrated gates. |

## B894 AF_PACKET Fanout Semantics Evidence

| Row | Evidence | Remaining |
|---|---|---|
| 41 `socket`, 54 `setsockopt` | Packet-origin output suppresses its complete fanout group before selection while ordinary sockets suppress only the origin. The group hook alone applies `PACKET_FANOUT_FLAG_IGNORE_OUTGOING`; a selected member's `PACKET_IGNORE_OUTGOING` does not suppress fanout delivery. Final release uses Linux swap-delete ordering. Packet-ring replacement validates before unlink, then unlinks and appends a running member under one membership/delivery lock order; rejected changes preserve member order. Hosted tests cover LB, CPU, QM, CBPF, EBPF, close order, BPF retention, successful and rejected ring replacement, group suppression, and ordinary observers. Four new GNU/glibc records (`origin_suppresses_group`, `member_ignore_not_group_flag`, `group_ignore_outgoing`, `close_swap_delete`) match host Linux exactly in the x86 84-record differential. Hosted net passes 860/860 and both kernel targets build. | Existing non-AF_PACKET socket and setsockopt row gaps remain. N07.10 retains queue charging, hardware timestamps, loopback classification, duplicate V3 publication, expanded differential cases, aarch64 execution, and integrated gates. |

## B903 AF_PACKET TX Poll Evidence

| Syscall rows | Evidence added | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| 7 `poll`, 23 `select`, 232 `epoll_wait`, 270 `pselect6`, 271 `ppoll`, 281 `epoll_pwait`, 441 `epoll_pwait2` | The canonical AF_PACKET socket poll mask preserves generic datagram `POLL_OUT` readiness for available, `SEND_REQUEST`, `SENDING`, and `WRONG_FORMAT` TX-ring states. TX-ring status notifications are keyed to `POLL_OUT`, so read-only subscribers are not spuriously woken. Hosted tests cover every state, a real completion, and subscriber filtering. The GNU/glibc probe obtains kernel-generated `WRONG_FORMAT` from an invalid TX offset, observes readiness, repairs and republishes the frame, and completes it. The full TX record matches host Linux byte-for-byte in the x86 84-record differential. Focused TX tests pass 11/11, full net passes 860/860, both GNU targets compile, and x86_64/aarch64 kernel builds pass. | Row statuses are unchanged. Broader send-buffer pressure, device-down readiness, exact epoll ready-list semantics, and non-AF_PACKET row-specific ABI/error-ordering gaps remain. B948 removes the final AF_PACKET differential mismatches. |

## B925 AF_PACKET Queue Truesize Evidence

| Syscall rows | Evidence added | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| 41 `socket`, 45 `recvfrom`, 47 `recvmsg`, 54 `setsockopt`, 55 `getsockopt`, 299 `recvmmsg` | The canonical non-ring AF_PACKET queue retains Linux 6.19 64-bit skb allocation-class charge instead of Rust object/payload size. Admission tests current rmem before charging, so the frame crossing `SO_RCVBUF` is accepted and the next frame drops; dequeue releases the exact retained charge. Copy fallback and fanout rollover consume the same charge model. Hosted tests cover exact linear/paged classes, crossing admission, pressure clearing, and destructive packet/drop statistics. A GNU/glibc probe with effective `SO_RCVBUF=4096` proves Linux and Oxide both accept five 64-byte frames and drop the sixth. Full net passes 861/861, both GNU targets compile, both kernel targets build, and the x86 85-record differential differs only in the three existing N07.10.8 RX-ring records. | Row statuses remain unchanged. Broader `SO_RCVBUF` semantics, ring publication, security hooks, and non-AF_PACKET row audits remain. B943 closes production timestamp ingress; B948 closes the recorded RX-ring mismatches. |

## Immediate ABI Drift Found From Linux Source

- `336:uprobe` exists in upstream Linux source; current Oxide route status is `NEEDS-AUDIT` with route `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:141`.
- `470:listns` exists in upstream Linux source; current Oxide route status is `NEEDS-AUDIT` with route `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:134`.
- `471:rseq_slice_yield` exists in upstream Linux source; current Oxide route status is `NEEDS-AUDIT` with route `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:135`.

N10 `recvmsg` update: `B1067-network-recvmsg` propagates ancillary-control
usercopy `EFAULT` through inet, AF_UNIX, and netlink receive callers instead of
silently treating a failed cmsg copy as truncation. Row 47 remains `PARTIAL`
pending protocol-specific errors and ancillary data, true OOB, VSOCK parity,
security hooks, compat ABI, and syscall-context differential coverage.

N11 `recvmmsg` update: `B1068-network-recvmmsg` resolves the pinned socket
before importing the relative timeout, preserving Linux `EBADF` precedence over
an invalid timeout pointer; `net_common` source-contract tests cover the order.
Row 299 remains `PARTIAL` pending compat layout, restart semantics, complete
batch fault/error behavior, security hooks, and differential coverage.

N12 `shutdown` update: `B1069-network-shutdown` applies connected UDP
`SHUT_RD` to both owned IPv4 and IPv6 receive queues; row 48 remains `PARTIAL`
pending complete family, error-ordering, security, and differential audits.

N13 `bind` update: `B1070-network-bind` validates the complete requested
sockaddr readable range after fd lookup before family-specific parsing, closing
the AF_PACKET and related volatile-read fault path. Row 49 remains `PARTIAL`
pending family policy, reuse/TIME_WAIT, security, and differential audits.
